<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Dillan wrote down how many pages he read over the past $5$ days.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


What is the median of the number of pages which he read?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the pages read in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Pages read}           &= 70, \quad 60, \quad 70, \quad 30, \quad 90 \\\\
\text{Ordered pages read}   &= 30, \quad 60, \quad \maroonC
{70}, \quad 70, \quad 90 \\\\
\text{Median}               &= 70
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$70$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
Let's arrange the pages read in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Pages read}           &= 70, \quad 60, \quad 70, \quad 30, \quad 90 \\\\
\text{Ordered pages read}   &= 30, \quad 60, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{70}, \quad 70, \quad 90 \\\\
\text{Median}               &= 70
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$70$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Some students compared the number of pets which they had. The results are displayed below.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Find the range of the number of pets owned.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Range}   &= 9 - 2 \\\\
                &= 7
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>

<div class='question question'>

The number of students absent from Year $7$ was recorded as follows.

| Monday | Tuesday | Wednesday | Thursday | Friday |
|:------:|:-------:|:---------:|:--------:|:------:|
| $4$    | $6$     | $5$       | $3$      | $?$    |

If the **mean number** of students absent that week from Year $7$, was $4$, how many students were absent on Friday?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the number of students absent on Friday $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {4 + 6 + 5 + 3 + a} {5}          &= 4 \\\\
\dfrac {18 + a} {5}                     &= 4 \\\\
18 + a                                  &= 4 \times 5 \\\\
18 + a                                  &= 20 \\\\
a                                       &= 20 - 18 \\\\
a                                       &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

Number of students absent on Friday $= 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the number of students absent on Friday $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {4 + 6 + 5 + 3 + a} {5}          &= 4 \\\\
\dfrac {18 + a} {5}                     &= 4 \\\\
18 + a                                  &= 4 \times 5 \\\\
18 + a                                  &= 20 \\\\
a                                       &= 20 - 18 \\\\
a                                       &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of students absent on Friday $= 2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Find the mean weight of the sharks below.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 
![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 
![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 
![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 
![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 
![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 
![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 
![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean weight}  &= \dfrac {3 + 6 + 9 + 22 + 28 + 1 + 6 + 5} {8} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {80} {8} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel{8}} {\cancel{8}} \\\\
                    &= 10  \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10  \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean weight}  &= \dfrac {3 + 6 + 9 + 22 + 28 + 1 + 6 + 5} {8} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {80} {8} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel{8}} {\cancel{8}} \\\\
                    &= 10  \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$10  \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Which set of numbers have a total of $14$ and a range of $3$?

-  $3,5,6$
-  $4,4,6$
-  $4,4,7$
-  $4,5,6$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


$3, 5, 6$

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total}\       &= 3 + 5 + 6 \\\\
                    &= 14 \\\\
\text{Range}\       &= 6 - 3 \\\\
                    &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3, 6, 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_range
<emptyline>
$3, 5, 6$
<emptyline>
Let's verify.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total}\       &= 3 + 5 + 6 \\\\
                    &= 14 \\\\
\text{Range}\       &= 6 - 3 \\\\
                    &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3, 6, 5$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>DFFF02BA-14B2-46C5-8365-D64032206A37</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1:1:19</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tstatistics
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

$4$ girls have an average height of $1.4 \ \text{m}$. A fifth girl of height $1.2 \ \text{m}$ joins them.
What is the average height of all five girls?

-  $2.6 \ \text{m}$
-  $1.3 \ \text{m}$
-  none of these
-  $1.36 \ \text{m}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total height of 4 girls}     &= 4 \times 1.4 \\\\
                                    &= 5.6 \ \text {m} \\\\
\text {Average height of 5 girls}   &= \dfrac {5.6 + 1.2} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {6.8} {5} \\\\
                                    &= 1.36 \ \text {m}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$1.36 \ \text {m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total height of 4 girls}     &= 4 \times 1.4 \\\\
                                    &= 5.6 \ \text {m} \\\\
\text {Average height of 5 girls}   &= \dfrac {5.6 + 1.2} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {6.8} {5} \\\\
                                    &= 1.36 \ \text {m}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>D</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1.36 \ \text {m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</li>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

The mean of two numbers is six. One of the numbers 
is minus two. What is the other number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the other number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a + (-2)} {2}   &= 6 \\\\
a - 2                   &= 6 \times 2 \\\\
a - 2                   &= 12 \\\\
a                       &= 12 + 2 \\\\
a                       &= 14
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the other number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

a - 2                   &= 6 \times 2 \\\\
a - 2                   &= 12 \\\\
a                       &= 12 + 2 \\\\
a                       &= 14
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Gita threw three darts.

Use the information in the box to work out what numbers she threw.

- The lowest number was $10$.
- The range was $10$.
- The mean was $15$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


Let the highest number $= h$

Let the middle number $= m$

As per the question, equating for range,

$
\begin {aligned}
10                  &= h - 10 \\\\
h                   &= 10 + 10 \\\\
h                   &= 20
\end {aligned}
$

Highest number $= 20$

As per the question, equating for mean,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {h + m + 10} {3}     &= 15 \\\\
\dfrac {20 + m + 10} {3}    &= 15 \\\\
30 + m                      &= 15 \times 3 \\\\
30 + m                      &= 45 \\\\
m                           &= 45 - 30 \\\\
m                           &= 15
\end {aligned}
$

Middle number $= 15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_range
<emptyline>
Let the highest number $= h$
<emptyline>
Let the middle number $= m$
<emptyline>
As per the question, equating for range,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
10                  &= h - 10 \\\\
h                   &= 10 + 10 \\\\
h                   &= 20
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Highest number $= 20$
<emptyline>
As per the question, equating for mean,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {h + m + 10} {3}     &= 15 \\\\
\dfrac {20 + m + 10} {3}    &= 15 \\\\
30 + m                      &= 15 \times 3 \\\\
30 + m                      &= 45 \\\\
m                           &= 45 - 30 \\\\
m                           &= 15
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Middle number $= 15$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean score}  &= \dfrac {3 + 4 + 2 + 0 + 1 + 8} {6} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {18} {6} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 6} {6} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {6}} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
                    &= 3
\end {aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

a                                       &= 10
\end {aligned}
$

Goals scored in the seventh game $= 10$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A set of four numbers $5, 7, 12$ and $X$ have a mean of $10$. What is $X$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
10            &= \dfrac {5 + 7 + 12 + X} {4} \\\\
10 \times 4   &= 5 + 7 + 12 + X \\\\
40            &= 24 + X \\\\
24 + X        &= 40 \\\\
X             &= 40 - 24 \\\\
X             &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

Value of X $= 16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16$

</div>

X             &= 40 - 24 \\\\
X             &= 16
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Value of X $= 16$
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The mean length.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean length}     &= \dfrac {305 \ \text{cm} + 2.95 \ \text{m} + 225 \ \text{cm} + 3.6 \ \text{m} + 3.15 \ \text{m}} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3.05 \ \text{m} + 2.95 \ \text{m} + 2.25 \ \text{m} + 3.6 \ \text{m} + 3.15 \ \text{m}} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {15} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                        &= 3
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>

$5, 24, 37, 45, 36, 39, 22$ and $16$

</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the mean (average) number of passangers.

Show how you worked this out.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}\  &= \dfrac {5 + 24 + 37 + 45 + 36 + 39 + 22 + 16} {8} \\\\
              &= \dfrac {224} {8} \\\\
              &= \dfrac {8 \times 28} {8} \\\\
              &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 28} {\cancel 8} \\\\
              &= 28
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$28$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}\  &= \dfrac {5 + 24 + 37 + 45 + 36 + 39 + 22 + 16} {8} \\\\
              &= \dfrac {224} {8} \\\\
              &= \dfrac {8 \times 28} {8} \\\\
              &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 28} {\cancel 8} \\\\
              &= 28
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$28$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

Work out the range of the number of passangers.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Range}\   &= 45 - 5 \\\\
                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

She worked out that the median number of passangers was $30$. Show what working she used to get this answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the numbers in ascending order.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Numbers}\            &= 5, \quad 24, \quad 37, \quad 45, \quad 36, \quad 39, \quad 22, \quad 16 \\\\
\text{Ordered numbers}\    &= 5, \quad 16, \quad 22, \quad \maroonC
{24}, \quad \maroonC
{36}, \quad 37, \quad 39, \quad 45 \\\\
\text{Median}\             &= \dfrac {24 + 36} {2} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {60} {2} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {2 \times 30} {2} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 30} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                           &= 30 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
Let's arrange the numbers in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Numbers}\            &= 5, \quad 24, \quad 37, \quad 45, \quad 36, \quad 39, \quad 22, \quad 16 \\\\
\text{Ordered numbers}\    &= 5, \quad 16, \quad 22, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{24}, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{36}, \quad 37, \quad 39, \quad 45 \\\\
\text{Median}\             &= \dfrac {24 + 36} {2} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {60} {2} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {2 \times 30} {2} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 30} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                           &= 30 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$30$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question question'>

Andrew went on a cycling holiday.

The table shows how far he cycled each day. 

|       Monday      |      Tuesday      |     Wednesday     |      Thursday     |
|:-----------------:|:-----------------:|:-----------------:|:-----------------:|
| $32.3\ \text{km}$ | $38.7\ \text{km}$ | $43.5\ \text{km}$ | $45.1\ \text{km}$ |

He claimed; 'On average, I cycled over $\bold{40 \ \text{km}}$ a day'. 

Show that Andrew is wrong.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}         &= \dfrac {32.3 + 38.7 + 43.5 + 45.1} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {159.6} {4} \\\\
                    &= 39.9 \neq 40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$39.9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}         &= \dfrac {32.3 + 38.7 + 43.5 + 45.1} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {159.6} {4} \\\\
                    &= 39.9 \neq 40
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$39.9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p>city-of-london-school--11-plus--maths--2008--sample-paper-group-2:1:5</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tstatistics
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Which two numbers have a **mean** of $\bold{10}$ and a **range** of $\bold{8}$?

The numbers are $\Box$ and $\Box$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


Let the first number $= a$

Second number $= 8 + a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a + 8 + a} {2}  &= 10 \\\\
a + a + 8               &= 10 \times 2 \\\\
2a + 8                  &= 20 \\\\
2a                      &= 20 - 8 \\\\
2a                      &= 12 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                       &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Second number}    &= 6 + 8 \\\\
                        &= 14
\end{aligned}
$

Numbers $= 6, 14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6, 14$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_range
<emptyline>
Let the first number $= a$
<emptyline>
Second number $= 8 + a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a + 8 + a} {2}  &= 10 \\\\
a + a + 8               &= 10 \times 2 \\\\
2a + 8                  &= 20 \\\\
2a                      &= 20 - 8 \\\\
2a                      &= 12 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 6} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                       &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Second number}    &= 6 + 8 \\\\
                        &= 14
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Numbers $= 6, 14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6, 14$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Suki saves $\pounds 12$ in January, $\pounds 18$ in February and $\pounds 5$ in March. 

What is her mean (average) monthly saving? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average monthly saving}  &= \dfrac {12 + 18 + 5} {3} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {35} {3} \\\\
                               &= \pounds 11.67
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 11.67$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean

$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many pupils were doing homework?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Pupils doing homework}        &= \dfrac {60} {360} \times 90 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {60 \times 90} {360} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {60 \times 6 \times 15} {60 \times 6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {60} \times \cancel 6 \times 15} {\cancel {60} \times \cancel 6} \\\\
                                    &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {60} \times \cancel 6 \times 15} {\cancel {60} \times \cancel 6} \\\\
                                    &= 15
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$10$ pupils went to the youth club.

What is the size of the angle for that sector of the chart?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Angle of youth club sector}           &= \dfrac {10} {90} \times 360 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10 \times 360} {90} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10 \times 90 \times 4} {90} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {90} \times 4} {\cancel {90}} \\\\
                                            &= 10 \times 4 \\\\
                                            &= 40 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Angle of youth club sector}           &= \dfrac {10} {90} \times 360 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10 \times 360} {90} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10 \times 90 \times 4} {90} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {90} \times 4} {\cancel {90}} \\\\
                                            &= 10 \times 4 \\\\
                                            &= 40 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='topics'>

tstatistics
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Four boys played in a cricket match. Their batting scores were $26, 13, 4$ and $19$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What was the average (mean) score?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean} &= \dfrac {26 + 13 + 4 + 19} {4} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {62} {4} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {62 \times 25} {4 \times 25} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {1550} {100} \\\\
            &= 15.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean} &= \dfrac {26 + 13 + 4 + 19} {4} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {62} {4} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {62 \times 25} {4 \times 25} \\\\
            &= \dfrac {1550} {100} \\\\
            &= 15.5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Another boy, Sam, batted next. The average (mean) score for the five boys is now $16$. What was Sam’s score?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let Sam's score $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {26 + 13 + 4 + 19 + a} {5}           &= 16 \\\\
\dfrac {62 + a} {5}                         &= 16 \\\\
62 + a                                      &= 16 \times 5 \\\\
62 + a                                      &= 80 \\\\
a                                           &= 80 - 62 \\\\
a                                           &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Score of Sam $= 18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let Sam's score $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {26 + 13 + 4 + 19 + a} {5}           &= 16 \\\\
\dfrac {62 + a} {5}                         &= 16 \\\\
62 + a                                      &= 16 \times 5 \\\\
62 + a                                      &= 80 \\\\
a                                           &= 80 - 62 \\\\
a                                           &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Score of Sam $= 18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

In the grid below each number in the middle of a row or column is the average (mean) of the numbers on either side of it.

What value must be placed in the shaded box?

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number in the 2nd box}                &= \dfrac {0.2 + 1.2} {2} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {1.4} {2} \\\\
                                            &= 0.7 \\\\
\text{Number in the 8th box}                &= \dfrac {1.4 + 2.2} {2} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3.6} {2} \\\\
                                            &= 1.8 \\\\
\text{Number in the shaded box}             &= \dfrac {0.7 + 1.8} {2} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2.5} {2} \\\\
                                            &= 1.25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$

                                            &= \dfrac {3.6} {2} \\\\
                                            &= 1.8 \\\\
\text{Number in the shaded box}             &= \dfrac {0.7 + 1.8} {2} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2.5} {2} \\\\
                                            &= 1.25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Angle of Archery sector}          &= 25 \% \ \text{of} \ 360 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25} {100} \times 360 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25 \times 360} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25 \times 4 \times 90} {25 \times 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 4 \times 90} {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 90 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

True

</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total members in archery}         &= 25 \% \ \text{of} \ 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25} {100} \times 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25 \times 60} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25 \times 4 \times 15} {25 \times 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 4 \times 15} {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 15 \\\\
\text{Total members in basketball}      &= 20 \% \ \text{of} \ 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {100} \times 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 60} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 5 \times 12} {20 \times 5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times \cancel 5 \times 12} {\cancel {20} \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                                        &= 12 \\\\
\text{Difference}                       &= 15 - 12 \\\\
                                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

False

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

False

</div>

                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 4 \times 15} {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 15 \\\\

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

True or false? Two thirds of the embers wanted to do either skating or archery.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total members in archery}         &= 25 \% \ \text{of} \ 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25} {100} \times 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25 \times 60} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25 \times 4 \times 15} {25 \times 4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 4 \times 15} {\cancel {25} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 15 \\\\
\text{Total members in skating}         &= 40 \% \ \text{of} \ 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {40} {100} \times 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {40 \times 60} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 2 \times 5 \times 12} {20 \times 5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 2 \times \cancel 5 \times 12} {\cancel {20} \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                                        &= 2 \times 12 \\\\
                                        &= 24 \\\\
\text{Sum of both}                      &= 24 + 15 \\\\
                                        &= 39 \\\\
\text{Two thirds of total}              &= \dfrac {2} {3} \times 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 60} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 20} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 3 \times 20} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 2 \times 20 \\\\
                                        &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

False

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

False

</div>
</div>

                                        &= \dfrac {25 \times 60} {100} \\\\

                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 60} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 20} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 3 \times 20} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 2 \times 20 \\\\
                                        &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
False

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Petra asks five friends how much time they spent watching TV the day before. The answers were $2, 3, 4, 5$ and $8 \ \text{hours}$.

What is the average amount of time watching TV?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average time}     &= \dfrac {2 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 8} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {22} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {22 \times 2} {5 \times 2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {44} {10} \\\\
                        &= 4.4 \ \text{hours}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$

                        &= \dfrac {22} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {22 \times 2} {5 \times 2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {44} {10} \\\\
                        &= 4.4 \ \text{hours}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>

</div>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total time}\  &= (1 \times 5) + (2 \times 5) + (3 \times 6) + (4 \times 4) \\\\
                    &= 5 + 10 + 18 + 16 \\\\
                    &= 49 \ \text{hours}
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='uuid'>
<p>6CCBAA56-EE96-4328-95FF-B7D6CB2D9919</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-1:1:31</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_mr_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prcc</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tstatistics
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

The mean shoe size for six boys was $5$. Five of the boys had sizes $3, 7, 6, 2, 3$. What was the shoe size of the sixth boy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let shoe size of the sixth boy $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3 + 7 + 6 + 2 + 3 + a} {6}      &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {21 + a} {6}                     &= 5 \\\\
21 + a                                  &= 5 \times 6 \\\\
21 + a                                  &= 30 \\\\
a                                       &= 30 - 21 \\\\
a                                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

Shoe size of the sixth boy $= 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let shoe size of the sixth boy $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3 + 7 + 6 + 2 + 3 + a} {6}      &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {21 + a} {6}                     &= 5 \\\\
21 + a                                  &= 5 \times 6 \\\\
21 + a                                  &= 30 \\\\
a                                       &= 30 - 21 \\\\
a                                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Shoe size of the sixth boy $= 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Which day has the median temperature?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the pages read in ascending order.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Temperatures}\ &= -6, -5, 4, -2, -1 \\\\
\text{Ordered temperatures}\ &= -6. -5, \maroonC
{-2}, -1, 4 \\\\
\text{Median temperature} &= -2
\end{aligned}
$

Thursday has median temperature.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Thursday

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
Let's arrange the pages read in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Temperatures}\ &= -6, -5, 4, -2, -1 \\\\
\text{Ordered temperatures}\ &= -6. -5, \rtq_katex_color_info{-2}, -1, 4 \\\\
\text{Median temperature} &= -2
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Thursday has median temperature.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Thursday
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p>730AE050-56B3-4767-99F8-E08D92BFFFD3</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>devonport-high-school-for-boys--11-plus--maths--2018--sample-paper-3:1:33</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_mr_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prcc</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tstatistics
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

The mean of $6$ numbers is $9$. Five of the numbers are $3, 6, 7, 8, 6$. What is the $6 \text{th}$ number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let sixth number $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3 + 6 + 7 + 8 + 6 + a} {6}          &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac {30 + a} {6}                         &= 9 \\\\
30 + a                                      &= 6 \times 9 \\\\
30 + a                                      &= 54 \\\\
a                                           &= 54 - 30 \\\\
a                                           &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

Sixth number $= 24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let sixth number $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3 + 6 + 7 + 8 + 6 + a} {6}          &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac {30 + a} {6}                         &= 9 \\\\
30 + a                                      &= 6 \times 9 \\\\
30 + a                                      &= 54 \\\\
a                                           &= 54 - 30 \\\\
a                                           &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Sixth number $= 24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

The mean shoe size for six boys was $5$. Five of the boys had sizes $4, 5, 6, 7, 4$. What was the shoe size of the sixth boy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the size of sixth boy $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {4 + 5 + 6 + 7 + 4 + a} {6}      &= 5 \\\\
\dfrac {26 + a} {6}                     &= 5 \\\\
26 + a                                  &= 5 \times 6 \\\\
26 + a                                  &= 30 \\\\
a                                       &= 30 - 26 \\\\
a                                       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

The size of sixth boy $= 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the size of sixth boy $= a$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

26 + a                                  &= 5 \times 6 \\\\
26 + a                                  &= 30 \\\\
a                                       &= 30 - 26 \\\\
a                                       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The size of sixth boy $= 4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Peter Pilgrim has scored a goal in each of his last $4$ football games. On average he has taken $23$ minutes to score.

He scores again in the next game after $33 \ \text{minutes}$. 

What will the average time to score be for all five games?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average time}\    &= \dfrac {4 \times 23 + 33} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {92 + 33} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {125} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 25} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 25} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                        &= 25 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$225 \ \text{minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean

                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 25} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 25} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                        &= 25 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$225 \ \text{minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='workings'>

|   $7$     |    $2$    |

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the mean (average) of the number of chocolate coins.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mean}                &= \dfrac { 8 + 7 + 11 + 6 + 9 + 7} {6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {48} {6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {6 \times 8} {6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 8} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                            &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean

$

                            &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the range of quantities in the $6$ bags.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Range}   &= 11 - 6 \\\\
                &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_range
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

                &= 5

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children who travelled to USA}        &= \dfrac {45} {360} \\\\

<div class='workings'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

After his French mark are announced, Darshan's average score from all six tests falls to $6.5$ marks. 
Calculate the mark he scored in his French test.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the marks in French $=a$,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {9 + 6 + 8 + 4 + 8 + a} {6}      &= 6.5 \\\\
\dfrac {35 + a} {6}                     &= 6.5 \\\\
35 + a                                  &= 6.5 \times 6 \\\\
35 + a                                  &= 39 \\\\
a                                       &= 39 - 35 \\\\
a                                       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Marks scored in French test $= 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean

35 + a                                  &= 6.5 \times 6 \\\\
35 + a                                  &= 39 \\\\
a                                       &= 39 - 35 \\\\
a                                       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Marks scored in French test $= 4$
</code></pre>

</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The pass mark for each test is $50\%$. Write down what fraction of Darshan's six tests (including his French) he managed to pass, leaving your answer in its simplest form.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total Subjects}\  &= 6 \\\\
\text{Passed subjects}\ &= 4 \\\\
\text{Fraction}\        &= \dfrac {\text{Passed subjects}} {\text{Total subjects}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {4} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 2} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {2} } {3 \times \cancel {2} } \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {3}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total Subjects}\  &= 6 \\\\
\text{Passed subjects}\ &= 4 \\\\
\text{Fraction}\        &= \dfrac {\text{Passed subjects}} {\text{Total subjects}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {4} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 2} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {2} } {3 \times \cancel {2} } \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {2} {3}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {2} {3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In the end term tests, Darshan scores $15$ out of $20$ in his History.

Work out what his percentage improvement is in this subject.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Midterm's percentage marks}   &= \dfrac {4} {10} \times 100 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 100} {10} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 10\cancel0} {1\cancel0} \\\\
                                    &= 4 \times 10 \% \\\\
                                    &= 40 \% \\\\
\text{Endterm's percentage marks}   &= \dfrac {15} {20} \times 100 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {15 \times 100} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {15 \times 5 \times 20} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {15 \times 5 \times \cancel {20}} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                    &= 15 \times 5 \% \\\\
                                    &= 75 \% \\\\
\text{Percentage improvement}\      &= 75 \% - 40 \% \\\\
                                    &= 35\% \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35\%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Midterm's percentage marks}   &= \dfrac {4} {10} \times 100 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 100} {10} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 10\cancel0} {1\cancel0} \\\\
                                    &= 4 \times 10 \% \\\\
                                    &= 40 \% \\\\
\text{Endterm's percentage marks}   &= \dfrac {15} {20} \times 100 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {15 \times 100} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {15 \times 5 \times 20} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {15 \times 5 \times \cancel {20}} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                    &= 15 \times 5 \% \\\\
                                    &= 75 \% \\\\
\text{Percentage improvement}\      &= 75 \% - 40 \% \\\\
                                    &= 35\% \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$35\%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

On the five school days of a given week, Joshua recorded how many minutes late his school bus arrived each morning. Here is what he recorded:

|    Day    |         Time         |
|:---------:|:--------------------:|
|   Monday  |  $5 \text{ minutes}$ |
|  Tuesday  |  $8 \text{ minutes}$ |
| Wednesday |  $4 \text{ minutes}$ |
|  Thursday | $10 \text{ minutes}$ |
|   Friday  |  $8 \text{ minutes}$ |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down the mode of the amount of minutes the bus was late over the week. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

|       Time       |  Count  |
|:----------------:|:-------:|
| $4 \ \text{min}$ |   $1$   |
| $5 \ \text{min}$ |   $1$   |
| $8 \ \text{min}$ |   $2$   |
| $10 \ \text{min}$|   $1$   |

Mode $= 8 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>|       Time       |  Count  |
|:----------------:|:-------:|
| $4 \ \text{min}$ |   $1$   |
| $5 \ \text{min}$ |   $1$   |
| $8 \ \text{min}$ |   $2$   |
| $10 \ \text{min}$|   $1$   |
<emptyline>
Mode $= 8 \ \text{min}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8 \ \text{min}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the range of the five times that Joshua recorded.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Range} &= 10 - 4 \\\\
              &= 6  \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate the mean average time that the bus was late over the course of the week.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mean} &= \dfrac {5 + 8 + 4 + 10 + 8} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {35} {5} \\\\
             &= 7  \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$7  \ \text{min}$

$

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The following Monday, Joshua again records how many minutes late the bus arrives, combining this with his results 
from the previous week, the mean average late time is now $6 \ \text{minutes}$.

How much was the bus late by on that second Monday?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the delay on second Monday $=a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {5 + 8 + 4 + 10 + 8 + a} {6}         &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {35 + a} {6}                         &= 6 \\\\
35 + a                                      &= 6 \times 6 \\\\
a                                           &= 36 - 35 \\\\
a                                           &= 1 \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1 \ \text{min}$

</div>

35 + a                                      &= 6 \times 6 \\\\
a                                           &= 36 - 35 \\\\
a                                           &= 1 \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Joshua keeps recording this information each morning. By the end of Thursday of the second week, the mean average has now fallen to 
$4 \ \text{minutes}$.

Explain what this tells us about the number of minutes that the bus was late over the middle three days of the second week.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total for middle three days $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {36 + a} {9}     &= 4 \\\\
36 + a                  &= 4 \times 9 \\\\
36 + a                  &= 36 \\\\
a                       &= 36 - 36 \\\\
a                       &= 0 \ \text{min}
\end{aligned}
$

Number of minutes bus was late over the middle three days $= 0 \ \text{min}$

</code></pre>
</div>
